Through the power of mental corruption, clinging to their own views, many holders of wrong view are reborn in the great hell at the time of the Twin Miracle and on other occasions. Thus, the opportune moment for the attainment of a wholesome state is indeed hard to obtain.

“Dullabhañca manussattaṃ, buddhuppādo ca dullabho;

Dullabhā khaṇasampatti, saddhammo paramadullabho”ti.

Rare is human birth, rare is the arising of a Buddha; rare is the opportune moment, exceedingly rare is the true Dhamma.

Imissā gāthāya attho vuttoyeva.

The meaning of this verse has already been stated.

Iti sāgarabuddhittheraviracite sīmavisodhane

Thus, in the Sīmāvisodhana composed by the Elder Sāgarabuddhi.

Pakiṇṇakakaṇḍo nāma pañcamo paricchedo.

The Miscellaneous Section, the fifth chapter.

Evaṃ satthuparinibbānato vassasataccayena patiṭṭhite isinā kāritattā’ isinagara’nti laddhanāme dvattapādibhūpālānaṃ nivāsaṭṭhānabhūte sīrikhettanagare erāvatiyā nadiyā pārimatīrabhūte pabbatasānumhi patiṭṭhitassa kañcanavaramahāthūpassa dāyakassa satvivamahādhammarañño kāle sāsanassa dvisahassasatādhikaekatiṃsatime vasse sāsane paṭiladdhasaddhānaṃ kulaputtānaṃ mahājānikaraṇavasena vimativinodaniyā vuttavacanaṃ saddayuttiatthayuttivasena sādhukaṃ avicinitvā nadiyā udakukkhepaṃ akatvā upasampadā kammassa kāritattā sāsane parājayamāpanne kulaputte upārambhakaraṇavasena sīmavipattihārako sīmasampattippakāsako gantho pavattati.

Thus, in the city of Sīrikhetta—which was established one hundred years after the Teacher’s Parinibbāna, gained the name 'Isinagara' because it was built by a sage, and was the residence of kings like Dvattapādi—during the time of King Satviva Mahādhammarāja, the donor of the excellent great golden stupa established on the mountain slope on the far bank of the Erāvati River, in the 2,131st year of the Dispensation: because clansmen who had regained faith in the Dispensation, by way of causing great loss, had the ordination act performed without carefully examining the words spoken in the Vimativinodanī in terms of grammar, logic, and meaning, and without performing the water-casting into the river, this treatise, which removes boundary defects and proclaims boundary correctness, proceeds for the purpose of rebuking those clansmen who had brought about failure in the Dispensation.

Ettāvatā ca sirikhettanagaragocaragāmena satvivamahādhammarājagurubhūtena mahāveyyākaraṇena tipiṭakadharena’saddhammakovido’ti pākaṭa nāmadheyyena mahātherena upajjhāyo hutvā paññādhipatīnaṃ nivāsabhūte pacchimajinacakkasāsanavare vāsigaṇācariyena gaṇavācakena vinayadharena mahāsāminā ca, rājagurunā tipiṭakanāgattherena ca ācariyo hutvā vejjakammajaṅghapesanakammādivasena anesanaṃ pahāya sammā ājīvena visuddhājīvehi saṅghagaṇehi kārakasaṅghā hutvā sirikhettanagarassa dakkhiṇadisābhāge dīghapabbatasānumhi erāvatiyā nadiyā tīre vālikapuḷine sīmāpekkhāya saha udakukkhepaṃ [M 0.0115] katvā pavattāya udakukkhepasīmāya dasadhā byañjanavipattiṃ akatvā ṭhānārahena ñatticatutthena kammena upasampadāya vīsativassena sāgarabuddhīti garūhi gahitanāmadheyyena bhikkhunā racito sāsanavipattihārako sīmavisodhanī nāma gantho samatto.

Thus, the treatise named Sīmāvisodhanī, which removes defects in the Dispensation, is completed. It was composed by the monk in his twentieth year whose name, Sāgarabuddhi, was given by his teachers. The preceptor was the great elder from the village region of Sirikhetta city, the royal teacher of King Satviva Mahādhammarāja, a great grammarian, a bearer of the Tipiṭaka, and renowned as 'Saddhammakovida'. The teachers were the great master Vāsigaṇācariya—a reciter for the group, a Vinaya holder, and the abode for those with predominant wisdom in the noble dispensation of the western Jina-wheel—and the royal teacher, the elder Tipiṭakanāga. The performing Sangha was comprised of communities of monks of pure livelihood, who lived by right livelihood, having abandoned wrong seeking through medical practice, messenger work, and so forth. The higher ordination was performed by means of a proper motion-and-three-announcements act, without the ten kinds of defects in phrasing, on a water-boundary established by performing the water-casting together with the boundary inspection on a sandy bank on the shore of the Erāvati River, on the long mountain slope in the southern quarter of Sirikhetta city.

Ettāvatā vibhattā hi, sappabhedappavattikā;

Sīmavisodhanīhesā, nipuṇā sādhucintitā.

Thus far, indeed, has this Sīmāvisodhanī been analyzed, with its various divisions and applications; it is subtle and well-considered.

Sirikhettāti paññāte, pure aparanāmake;

Dakkhiṇeyyadisābhāge, uccanena katālaye.

In the city known as Sirikhetta, which has another name, in the southern quarter, in a dwelling made on high ground.

Vasanto bhikkhu nāmena, sāgarabuddhīti vissuto;

Puṇṇe vīsativassamhi, ganthoyaṃ sādhucintito.

Dwelling there, the monk renowned as Sāgarabuddhi well-composed this treatise upon the completion of his twentieth year.

Mayāyaṃ racito gantho, niṭṭhappatto anākulo;

Evaṃ pāṇinaṃ sabbe, sīghaṃ sijjhantu saṅkappā.

This treatise, composed by me, has been completed without confusion. In the same way, may all the aspirations of living beings be swiftly fulfilled.

Yāva buddhotināmampi lokajeṭṭhassa tādino;

Tāva tiṭṭhatu yaṃ gantho, sāsane hārayaṃ tamaṃ.

As long as the very name of the Buddha, the foremost in the world, the Such One, remains, so long may this treatise endure, dispelling darkness in the Dispensation.

Uddhaṃ yāva bhavaggā ca, adho yāva avīcito;

Samantā cakkavāḷesu, ye sattā pathavīcarā.

Up to the highest realm of existence, down to the Avīci hell, and throughout the surrounding world-systems, all beings who move upon the earth—

Tepi sabbe mayā hontu, samasamavipākino;

Ciraṃ jīvatu no rājā, sāsanassa ujjotako.

May all of them, through me, be partakers of the same result. Long live our king, the illuminator of the Dispensation.

Dibbanto rājadhammena, arogo saha ñātibhi;

Anena puññakammena, bhaveyyaṃ jātijātiyaṃ.

Ruling by the Dhamma of kings, free from illness, together with relatives; by this meritorious deed, may I be reborn in life after life.

Sāsanaṃ jotayantova, sakyaputtassa sāsane;

Yadā nassati saddhammo, andhībhūto mahītale;

Devaloke tadā hessaṃ, tusite ṭhānamuttameti.

Illuminating the Dispensation, in the Dispensation of the Sakyan Son; when the true Dhamma vanishes, and the earth is plunged into darkness, then may I be in the heavenly realm, in the supreme abode of Tusita.

Sīmavisodhanī niṭṭhitā.

The Sīmāvisodhanī is completed.
